barrels, down 1.13% year over year, the data showed. Inflows from South Korea fell 19.07% month over month and
41.33% year over year to 1.47 million barrels in August, while those from Taiwan dropped 0.11% month over month and 3.50% year over year to 589,984 barrels.
Imports from Singapore and Malaysia nearly halved month over month in August to 362,922 barrels and 318,893 barrels, respectively. On a year-over-year basis, volumes from Singapore and Malaysia fell 43.98% and 33.81%, respectively.
Only volumes from China rose month over month, rebounding to 623,949 barrels in August from zero in July. However, imports from China remained 7.72% lower year over year.
The Platts FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene outright price averaged $148.43/barrel in August, up from $139.22/b in July and $124.93/b in June.
MSO stocks As of Oct. 6, Australia's petroleum industry held 2.871 billion
liters of gasoil, 811 million liters of kerosene and 1.823 billion liters of gasoline under its Minimum Stockholding Obligation (MSO), DCCEEW data released Oct. 9 showed.
This equated to 31 days of cover for diesel, 30 days for kerosene and 43 days for gasoline -- 27%, 20% and 106% above current MSO requirements, respectively.
In March, Australia introduced a temporary 20% reduction in its MSO for gasoil and gasoline to address supply disruptions caused by the conflict in the Middle East.
The MSO requirement for diesel was reduced to 2.262 billion liters from 2.827 billion liters, while the requirement for gasoline was lowered to 885 million liters from 1.106 billion liters, data from the department showed.
Australia introduced the MSO framework in 2023 to strengthen domestic fuel security by requiring refiners and importers to maintain minimum inventories of key fuels within the country.
-- Shu ling Lee, Mei huey Ng
US DATA: Diesel stocks fall to five-month low on surging demand
Inventories drop to 20-week low
Consumption jumps to 30-week high
Arb to Europe closed
US nationwide ultra-low sulfur diesel stocks fell by 2.154 million barrels to 91.58 million barrels for the week ended Oct. 9, the lowest level since the week ended May 22, driven by a surge in demand, according to Energy Information Administration data released Oct. 15.
The sharp draw on inventories was underpinned by a significant increase in products supplied, a proxy for demand, which jumped by 673,000 b/d to 4.323 million b/d. This represents the highest weekly demand since the week ended March 13, a 30-week high.

October 15, 2026

The stock draw was led by the Gulf Coast, where inventories fell by 1.317 million barrels to a seven-week low of 34.1 million barrels. In contrast, West Coast stocks saw a modest build of 96,000 barrels to 8.996 million barrels, despite a sharp drop in regional production.
Platts assessed USGC ULSD outright at $4.6895/gallon, 15.54 cents/gallon lower on the day. This occurred amid the NYMEX contract monthly roll, a sharply backwardated Mo1/Mo2 market structure of 19.11 cents/gallon, and a potential easing of US sanctions against shadow-fleet tankers in a bid to lower freight costs.
Total US production also declined, falling by 121,000 b/d to 4.945 million b/d, dipping back below the symbolic 5 million b/d level. The largest regional drop occurred on the West Coast, where output fell 59,000 b/d to a 19-week low of 373,000 b/d. The main refining region, the Gulf Coast, also saw production ease, falling 27,000 b/d to a two-week low of 2.897 million b/d. The East Coast was the only region to see an increase, with production rising 18,000 b/d to a two-week high of 262,000 b/d.
The draw on domestic stocks came as exports fell significantly, down 391,000 b/d to a three-week low of 1.373 million b/d. The decline in shipments coincided with an unfavorable arbitrage opportunity to Europe. According to S&P Global Energy CERA, the USGC-to-Northwest Europe arb flow incentive averaged minus $1.68/b during the reporting period.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoil Daily Market Analysis
South Korea's GS Caltex sells 600,000 barrels of gasoil
India's MRPL offers 40,000 mt of 10 ppm sulfur highspeed diesel
The Asian ultra-low sulfur gasoil market was firmer Oct. 15 following attacks by Houthi militants on oil facilities in Riyadh.
Brokers pegged the front-month November-December Singapore gasoil swaps time spread at $3.40/barrel in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 15, narrowing from $3.93/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 14.
Houthi militants have attacked Saudi Arabian Oil Co.'s sites in Dhahran, home to the company's headquarters, and in Riyadh, while vowing to continue targeting the company's oil facilities, according to an Oct. 14 statement from the Yemen-based group.
An Aramco administrative building in Dhahran was targeted Oct. 13 and caught fire, the state-run Saudi Press Agency said Oct. 14, citing an official energy ministry source. There were no injuries or fatalities, SPA said.
On physical cargoes, the benchmark Platts-assessed cash differential for FOB Singapore gasoil with a maximum sulfur content of 10 parts per million declined 64 cents/b day over day to $1.52/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 14.
In tenders, South Korea's GS Caltex had sold 300,000 barrels

of 10 ppm sulfur gasoil for loading over Nov. 28-30 from Yeosu at a discount heard ranging from $1.50/b to $1.80/b to the November average of Mean of Platts Singapore 10 ppm sulfur gasoil assessments, FOB. The company had also sold a similarly sized cargo of 50 ppm sulfur gasoil for Nov. 28-30 loading at a discount heard at about $2.50/b to $2.60/b over the same benchmark.
Within the same tender, GS Caltex had offered another MR cargo of 10 ppm sulfur gasoil, which was not awarded, according to Asia-based market sources.
Elsewhere, India's MRPL offered 40,000 mt of 10 ppm sulfur high-speed diesel loading over Oct. 24-25 from New Mangalore via a tender closing Oct. 15, with same-day validity.
In statistical news, China's refined product exports reached a 45-month high of 7.16 million metric tons in September, data from the General Administration of Customs showed Oct. 14.
The volume was the highest since December 2022, when exports were at 7.7 million mt, GAC data showed. It increased 39.3% year over year and 19.2% from the recent high of 6.01 million mt in August.
Australia's middle distillate imports fell 11.3% month over month and 16.3% year over year to 17.82 million barrels in August, the latest preliminary data from the Department of Climate Change, Energy, the Environment and Water showed Oct. 15.
The country's gasoil imports fell 8.44% month over month and 11.74% year over year to 14.44 million barrels in August. Total imports in the first eight months of 2026 inched 3.39% higher year over year to 129.01 million barrels, the data showed.
The decline came despite higher imports from South Korea, the top supplier, which rose 5.4% month over month and 8.66% year over year to 6.15 million barrels.
As of Oct. 6, Australia's petroleum industry held 2.871 billion liters of gasoil, 811 million liters of kerosene and 1.823 billion liters of gasoline under its Minimum Stockholding Obligation, DCCEEW data released Oct. 9 showed.
This equated to 31 days of cover for diesel, 30 days for kerosene and 43 days for gasoline -- 27%, 20% and 106% above current MSO requirements, respectively.

USGC ULSD differential hits 11-week high
Brazil accelerates US diesel import volumes
US diesel inventories drop to 5-month lows
US Gulf Coast ULSD market dynamics shifted on Oct. 15 as price differentials climbed, outright values fell, and increased tanker activity indicated robust demand from Brazil.
Platts assessed the Gulf Coast ULSD differential at an 11.75 cents/gallon increase, bringing it to a 3.5 cents/gallon premium over futures, as the underlying futures contract shifted to December NYMEX. This marks the highest differential since Sept. 22, when it reached a 3.75 cents/gallon premium to futures.
Meanwhile, the USGC ULSD outright went the opposite direction, declining by 15.54 cents/gallon to settle at $4.6895/ gallon. This occurred amid the transition between NYMEX contract months, a sharply backwardated M1/M2 market structure of 19.11 cents/gallon, and a potential easing of US sanctions against shadow-fleet tankers in a bid to lower

freight costs. The Platts tankers team reported a ULSD fixture in the Oct. 15
session, with Brazilian state-led oil company Petrobras booking the Forever Conviviality for a USGC-East Coast South America voyage at Worldscale 450 points with a transatlantic option at w400.
Brazil has imported 3.6 million barrels of gasoil and diesel so far in October and is on pace to surpass the previous month's 4.2 million barrels, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
Meanwhile, US nationwide ultra low sulfur diesel stocks fell by 2.154 million barrels to 91.58 million barrels, the lowest level since the week ended May 22, driven by a surge in demand, according to Energy Information Administration data released Oct. 15 for the week ended Oct. 9.

Marine Fuel
LSFO available storage at Zhoushan rises 11.12% on month in September
LSFO ullage up 14% on year
China allocates third batch of 1.07 mil mt LSFO export quotas
Low sulfur fuel oil storage available for lease at China's regional hub of Zhoushan gained 11.12% in September to 125.9 million cubic meters according to Zhejiang Mercantile Exchange data.
On an annual basis, the LSFO ullage was up 14.35% year over year, the data showed.
Crude ullage showed no month-over-month change at 1.80 million cu m, while year-over-year, it was down 12.20%, according to the data.
For clean petroleum products, storage levels had remained largely the same, with jet, naphtha and gasoline availability remaining unchanged at 270,000 cu m, 660,000 cu m and 770,000 cu m, respectively, at the end of September, according to the data.
However, diesel ullage had expanded, breaking a three-month downward trend and gaining slightly by 0.51% month over month to 977,000 cu m, according to the ZME data.
Tank utilization at the Zhoushan bunkering port declined about 1% in September, primarily due to higher ullage of LSFO and diesel capacity. Total capacity remains steady at 13.3 MMcm.
Data from local energy information provider JLC showed that the LSFO at China's refiners was at 1.136 million metric tons in September, up 1.07% from a month earlier and 8.4% from a year earlier.
However, the output is unlikely to sustain in October, according

SINGAPORE DATA: September bunker sales slip to 3-month low
Total Q3 sales 4.9% higher on quarter
HSFO volumes drop 5.3% on month
Elevated prices cap LNG sales
Bunker fuel sales at Singapore, the world's largest bunkering hub, dipped 0.8% year over year and fell nearly 1% month over month to a three-month low of 4.726 million metric tons in September, according to preliminary data from the Maritime and Port Authority of Singapore released late Oct. 14.
Total third-quarter bunker sales, however, rose 4.9% from the previous quarter.
Since last month, challenges in securing prompt cargoes among buyers extended beyond the low sulfur fuel oil grade to high sulfur fuel oil as well, in part resulting in the patchy demand seen lately.
The decline in Singapore's monthly bunker fuel sales, which

had risen over four consecutive months previously, was primarily led by a drop in the HSFO marine fuel grade.
Sales of high sulfur fuel oil, including the bioblended grade, fell 5.3% month over month to 1.993 million mt in September, despite increasing around 4% from the corresponding month last year.
The proportion of HSFO volumes stood at 42.2% of total bunker sales, down 1.9 percentage points from August but up 1.9 percentage points from September last year.
The Platts-assessed Singapore-delivered 380 CST HSFO bunker premium over FOB Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo assessments averaged at $49.19/mt in September, compared with $53.90/mt in August, according to data from Platts. However, the HSFO delivered bunker premium has since rebounded strongly in October, averaging at $74.29/mt over Oct. 1-14, amid depleting inventories and mounting difficulties in securing replenishment cargoes owing to climbing freight costs.
On the other hand, sales of the International Maritime Organization-compliant low sulfur fuel oil, including the bioblended grade, rose around 2.4% from the preceding month to 2.294 million mt in September. However, LSFO sales also dropped 5.3% from September last year.
LSFO sales made up 48.5% of total bunker sales, increasing 1.6 percentage points month over month but falling 2.3 percentage points year over year.
Platts assessed the Singapore-delivered 0.5%S marine fuel bunker premium over the FOB Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo values at an average of $61.80/mt over September, retreating from the August average of $90.10/mt. The LSFO delivered bunker premium has averaged $66.12/mt over Oct. 1-14, amid expectations of slightly lower volumes of arbitrage cargoes from Western markets arriving in Singapore this month compared with August.
Meanwhile, sales of low sulfur marine gasoil, which has a maximum sulfur content of 0.1%, rose 3% month over month to 383,170 mt, including the bio-blended grade. September's LSMGO sales, which saw a 1.4% increase from the same month last year, reached an eight-month high.
Total ship arrivals around the Singapore bunker hub slipped 0.2% from August to 11,608 but were still 5.6% higher compared with September 2025, MPA data also showed.
Alternative fuels demand mixed In the alternative fuels market, marine biofuel sales,
comprising of the bio-blended grades and neat biofuel, also known as B100, saw a steady recovery in volumes to 83,210 mt in September, marking a 45.1% rise month over month despite being 49% lower than the corresponding month in 2025.
Singapore LNG bunker sales fell 12.12% month over month to 51,500 mt in September but marked a 6.30% year-over-year rise, the latest MPA data showed.
LNG bunker demand across Asia remained capped by elevated prices, with market participants reporting limited activity in the spot market. Most demand was heard to be coming from buyers lifting volumes under existing term contracts.

The Platts-assessed Singapore delivered LNG bunker price and East China delivered LNG bunker spot price averaged $27.762/MMBtu and $27.757/MMBtu in September, at a premium of 80 cents/MMBtu to $1.50/MMBtu to the JKM, the benchmark price reflecting LNG delivered to Northeast Asia.
Weak spot demand weighed on premiums, with the LNG bunker premium to the JKM LNG benchmark falling to 80 cents/ MMBtu in the month on Sept. 14, marking its lowest level since Feb. 8, 2023, when the premium was 75 cents/MMBtu.
Asian LNG bunker prices also remained uncompetitive compared with conventional marine fuels, keeping price-sensitive segments such as bulk carriers and tankers largely away from the LNG bunker market, according to market participants.
The Platts-assessed Singapore-delivered LNG bunker fuel averaged a $5.258/GJ premium over Singapore-delivered 0.5% marine fuel in September.
Despite the month-on-month decline in September, Singapore LNG bunker sales remained relatively stable on a quarterly basis. Third-quarter sales totalled 168,800 mt, up 0.66% from 167,700 mt in the second quarter.

Resid
Rotterdam HSFO crack climb to four-month high amid higher dirty tanker freight
HSFO crack reaches 4-month high Dirty tanker freight rates elevated Resupply fears could tighten market
The Rotterdam front-month high sulfur fuel oil barge crack spread reached a four-month high on Oct. 14, as fears surrounding resupply emerged amid elevated dirty tanker freight rates.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month paper 3.5% sulfur fuel oil barge crack spread -- measuring the relative price of the product relative to the crude from which it is

made -- at minus $7.433/b on Oct. 14, up 62 cents/b day over day. This marked the highest level since June 10, when the spread
was assessed at minus $7.024/b. All prompt-month HSFO crack spreads climbed day over day. Traders attributed elevated freight rates and fears of resupply
to the upward movement of the crack. "Cracks have moved much higher, freight is the biggest
problem right now," a trader said. Arbitrage opportunities into the region from the Americas
were uneconomical due to elevated freight rates, another trader said.
Demand diverged in the physical high sulfur fuel oil market. In the Mediterranean, demand picked up in the west of the region following reports of tightness the week prior, traders said.
One Med-focused trader described the market as "balanced," adding that "I don't see a lack of product; you can easily find product in the West Med."
In Northwest Europe, demand remained limited, with market participants pointing to volatility in Brent futures and fluctuating ZRE-A ticket prices as the key headwinds in Rotterdam.
-- Joseph Jaffe
Platts Asia and Middle East Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
HSFO strength leads to narrower sulfur spread
Singapore bunker sales drop to 3-month low
The spread between 0.5% sulfur marine fuel and 380 CST high-sulfur fuel oil in Singapore continued to narrow on Oct. 15, led by continued strength in the HSFO market.
FOB Singapore 0.5% sulfur marine fuel commanded a premium of $46.10/mt over the 3.5% sulfur 380 CST grade at the Asian close on Oct. 14, down from a spread of $73.76/mt the previous day. The cash spread has been on a steep downward trend since September, Platts data showed.
In midafternoon trade on Oct. 15, the low-sulfur and highsulfur spread for November swaps narrowed to $70.50/mt, shrinking from the previous day's assessment of $84.55/mt.
The region continued to experience a supply shortage, with high tanker freight rates and sustained attacks on vessels at the Strait of Hormuz adding to uncertainty over flows to Asia, market sources said.
The narrowing sulfur spread made medium-sulfur fuel oil cargoes increasingly attractive for the HSFO blending pool, providing more options for the high-sulfur market but giving little reprieve to the LSFO market.
The LSFO market was not really short of medium-sulfur blending components to begin with, said a Singaporebased trader.

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
High freight rates hinder arbitrage opportunities
Mediterranean buying interest strengthens
VLSFO market remains balanced in Europe
Elevated dirty tanker freight rates continued to make arbitrage opportunities a challenge Oct. 15.
In the high sulfur fuel oil market, resupply concerns amid elevated dirty tanker freight rates emerged with market participants citing it as a factor behind the edging higher of the front-month Rotterdam barge crack spread on Oct. 14
One trader pointed to freight as the main driver, "Cracks have moved much higher, freight is the biggest problem right now."
Another noted that arbitrage opportunities from the Americas were uneconomical at current freight rates.
Physical demand diverged across the regions. In the Mediterranean, buying interest picked up in the west following reports of tightness the previous week, though one Med-focused trader described the market as balanced. In Northwest Europe, demand remained limited, with participants citing Brent futures volatility and fluctuating ZRE-A ticket prices as the key headwinds in Rotterdam. Supply-demand fundamentals in the very low sulfur fuel oil market remain well balanced across Northwest Europe and the Mediterranean. Recent strength in the paper market was attributed by participants to volumes fixed from Europe to Singapore and the Americas over the past week. A Northwest European trader said: "There were some arbs." Although dirty tanker freight rates remain elevated, some cargoes are moving on smaller vessels to avoid higher Suezmax rates. Like for high sulfur fuel oil, freight remains the main obstacle to keeping the arbitrage window open. In the European low sulfur fuel oil market, blending demand has held steady, while another participant described demand in the Mediterranean as slightly stronger. Trading activity in the Platts Market on Close assessment process was quiet on Oct. 15, with 2,000 mt trades observed in the Rotterdam VLSFO barge MOC and 12,000 mt traded for the

HSFO equivalent. There were no trades for fuel oil cargoes. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.

-- Joseph Jaffe

South Korea's Hanwha TotalEnergies seeks naphtha for H1 Dec delivery
Japan's Maruzen Petrochemical seeks naphtha for H2 Nov, H1 Dec delivery
Indian Oil offers 175,000 mt of naphtha for Nov-March loading
The Asian naphtha market remained firm Oct. 15, with spot tender activity for deliveries in both the second half of November and H1 December, as the current H2 November trading cycle winds down.
In the derivatives market, brokers pegged the balance-month October-November Mean of Platts Japan swaps time spread at $28/metric ton in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 15, down $2.75/ mt from the previous close.
In the spot market, South Korea's Hanwha TotalEnergies sought heavy full-range naphtha for H1 December delivery to Daesan, via a tender closed Oct.15. Japan's Maruzen Petrochemical has issued a tender to seek light naphtha for H2 November and H1 December delivery to Chiba. Both tenders closed Oct. 15, with same-day validity.
Cash premiums stayed firm for H1 December deliveries cargo with the conclusion of recent tenders, largely driven by higher freight rates.
South Korea's YNCC bought naphtha for December delivery to Yeosu via a tender closed Oct. 14. The H1 December cargo was awarded at a premium of about $17-$17.25/mt to Mean of Platts Japan naphtha assessments, CFR, with pricing over 45 days prior to delivery. In Japan, Mitsubishi Chemical purchased a 25,000-mt cargo of light naphtha to Kashima at a premium of about $20/mt to MOPJ assessments, CFR, with pricing over 45 days prior to delivery. The tender closed Oct. 13.
Over to India, Indian Oil has offered 175,000 mt of naphtha for Nov. 1-March 2 loading from Chennai. The tender also has an option to sell the five 35,000-mt cargoes individually over the same period. The tender will close Oct. 16, with same-day validity.

Platts European Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Cracking demand lifts naphtha prices
Propane-naphtha spread rises
The European naphtha complex was broadly stable Oct. 15, with no movement seen for the front-month crack while the prompt backwardation narrowed slightly.
Naphtha market participants pointed to an uptick in cracking demand from the petrochemical sector, which has underpinned prices over the past few days.
"Naphtha demand is good, prices are going up on cracking [demand]," a naphtha market participant said. "[It's] probably due to LPG prices."
A gasoline trading source said: "Naphtha seems to be hard to find."
The propane-naphtha spread has been rising significantly over the last week as propane prices rallied on limited North Sea supply and few inbound cargoes, making naphtha increasingly competitive.
The propane-naphtha spread is widely followed to determine which feedstock is more economical for cracking. While a naphtha baseline is needed, flexible steam crackers tend to switch to propane production when the spread falls below minus $60/metric ton.
The wider spread was driven by a strengthening propane complex, which saw lower North Sea production and less workable arbitrage economics into Northwest Europe, keeping October cargo availability scarce while inbound arrivals remained limited, according to multiple market participants.
The start of winter typically leads to a spike in propane prices due to increased buying from the residential sector as colder temperatures spark heating demand. The subsequent rise in price makes propane less attractive than competing feedstocks such as naphtha.
Meanwhile, clean freight continued to roof amid shipping attacks around Hormuz and the Bab al-Mandab Strait, with two additional vessel attacks reported in the Strait of Hormuz on Oct. 13 and at least five maritime incidents reported within 48 hours.
"Freight [is] indeed rocketing. [I'm] not sure it'll continue, but let's see," the first source said.
Platts assessed the physical naphtha CIF NWE cargo price at

$921.50/mt, up 50 cents/mt on the day. It assessed the frontmonth CIF NWE naphtha swap up $2/mt at $881.75/mt, while the crack was steady at minus $4.40/barrel.
Platts assessed the October/November time spread at $30/ mt, down $1/mt on the day, while the November/December spread was up $2.50/mt at $31/mt.
The front-month East-West spread -- the spread between the CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap and the CIF NWE equivalent -- was at $79.50/mt, down $5.75/mt on the day.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Barbara Fernandez-pita

Russia mulls partial ban on LPG exports: reports
LPG ban to apply to traders and small refiners
Gasoline imports to continue through 2026
Russia is considering introducing a partial ban on LPG exports until the end of 2026, Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak told local media on the sidelines of Russian Energy Week on Oct. 15.
The measures, currently being discussed by the energy ministry, would apply to non-producers, including traders and small refineries with up to 1 million metric tons/year of processing capacity, according to local media.
The partial ban could be enforced to ensure sufficient supply during repairs to Russian refineries, RBK Daily reported, citing sources.
LPG is used to some extent in Russia as an auto fuel, and demand has risen after large-scale refinery outages following Ukrainian drone strikes resulted in gasoline shortages, according to local media.
LPG equipment installations rose 30%-35% above typical levels, Kommersant daily reported during the summer.
Meanwhile, Novak said Oct. 15 that Russia will continue gasoline imports at least until the end of 2026, according to local media.
Novak said that imported gasoline accounts for around 10% of domestic consumption, without providing further details.
The Russian government and energy ministry did not immediately respond to requests for comment Oct. 15.
Russia has increased gasoline imports from Belarus and other countries to cover domestic shortages amid refinery outages.
The country also extended the full gasoline export ban until the end of January 2027.
-- Elza Turner
US DATA: Propane, propylene stocks drop to lowest since early August as exports retreat
Inventories draw 1.471 million barrels
Exports fall below 2 million b/d
Production falls to below 2.9 million b/d
US propane and propylene inventories fell below 107 million barrels for the first time since August during the week ended Oct. 9, as exports fell and overall demand exceeded analyst projections, according to US Energy Information Administration supply and demand data published Oct. 15.
Stocks tightened by 1.471 million barrels to 106.375 million barrels, their lowest level since early August, when stocks hovered at 104.974 million barrels. The decline marks the second

straight stock draw. The US Gulf Coast accounted for nearly the entire stock draw,
with regional stocks down 1.943 million barrels to 66.728 million barrels. Other regions built inventories, including PADD 1, which built 232,000 b/d.
Total demand, including exports and domestic product supplied, fell 36,000 barrels/day to 3.201 million b/d, according to EIA data. Of that total, exports dropped 472,000 b/d to 1.992 million b/d, falling below 2 million b/d for the first time since last September. Meanwhile, domestic product supplied jumped 436,000 b/d to 1.209 million b/d.
The stock draw beat out projections by S&P Global Energy CERA analysts, who projected a more minor 256,000-thousandbarrel stock draw based on the assumption that peak propane and propylene inventories have already been reached in 2026.
"This is significant because the 2026 inventory peak was observed in September, earlier than the typical seasonal high in late October," CERA analysts said in their Oct. 12 propane market forecast. "As a result, the 2026 and 2025 weekly inventory trends are likely to converge soon.
According to US LPG sources, the market expectation was for propane inventories to have a "small draw," in line with CERA analyst projections.
S&P Global Commodities at Sea shipping data showed a week-on-week export decline, though to only 2.2 million b/d for the week ended Oct. 9, versus the 1.992 million b/d in the EIA report for the same period.
Meanwhile, production volumes fell slightly to 2.898 million b/d, falling below 2.9 million b/d for the first time since early August.
Amid the inventory drawdown, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed US Gulf Coast propane at Mont Belvieu's Enterprise terminal up 1 cent/gallon to $1.0425/gal on Oct. 15. That's the highest price since Sept. 14, 2022.
-- Hunter Marrow
Indonesia adds 52,800 mt/y of LPG capacity at Cilamaya plant to curb imports
West Java plant produces 160 mt/day LPG
Indonesia imports 7 mil-8 mil mt/y LPG
Government explores CNG as cheaper option
Indonesia will add 52,800 metric tons/year of liquefied petroleum gas production capacity with the start of operations at local company PT Energi Nusantara Perkasa's Cilamaya plant in West Java, the company's President Director, Sardjono, said Oct. 15. The project could help Indonesia reduce its dependence on LNP imports.
The plant is designed to produce 160 metric tons/day of LPG and 320 barrels/day of condensate, Sardjono said at the inauguration, which marked the start of operations and production.
"We believe national energy independence must be built

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

31

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 15, 2026

through real investment, stronger domestic industry and synergy between the government and businesses," Sardjono said.
Indonesia produces no more than 1.8 million mt/year of LPG domestically, compared with annual consumption of 8.5 million mt, Energy and Mineral Resources Minister Bahlil Lahadalia said. Including industrial demand, total consumption rises to around 11 million-12 million mt/year, while imports amount to around 7 million-8 million mt/year, said Lahadalia, who also serves as Coordinating Minister for Downstreaming and Energy Transition.
Geopolitical conflicts heightened uncertainty over energy supplies and strengthened the case for countries to develop domestic resources, Lahadalia said.
"Almost every country in the world is now thinking about how to develop its own domestic energy sector," Lahadalia said, citing ongoing geopolitical conflicts.
The government is also exploring CNG as an alternative to LPG to leverage domestic gas resources and reduce reliance on imported fuel.
CNG is already used in some restaurants and hotels, while 3 kg cylinders are being tested and 12 kg cylinders are already in use, Lahadalia said. He said CNG could be 30%-40% cheaper, but did not specify the basis for the comparison.
Lahadalia said Indonesia's gas production was rich in methane and ethane, known as C1 and C2, which he said were not suitable for LPG production.
The Cilamaya facility has a gas processing capacity of 40 million cubic feet/day, with a 10% design allowance that could raise its maximum capacity to 44 MMcf/day. The remaining gas is returned to Pertamina's Offshore Northwest Java operations for further use, Sardjono said.
The plant uses mechanical refrigeration and turbine expander technology to improve LPG recovery. The relocation cost $12 million and the plant's assets were valued at $24 million, bringing the total investment in Cilamaya to around $36 million, Sardjono said.
ENP also operates an LPG processing plant in Gresik, East Java. The two facilities have a combined installed gas processing capacity of 140 MMcf/day. ENP has produced about 1 million mt of LPG over nearly 19 years of operating the Gresik plant, which has a capacity of 100 MMcf/day and has been run under a jointoperation arrangement with Pertamina since 2007, Sardjono said.
Sardjono asked the government to extend the Gresik plant's operating life, citing its contribution to domestic LPG production, and also called for reliable gas supplies, coordination among relevant stakeholders and technical evaluation to keep the facility operating.
Lahadalia instructed the ministry's oil and gas directorate general to expedite the extension process once the company had met all requirements, asking officials to put completed applications on his desk within a week for approval.
Lahadalia said LPG subsidies amounted to around 87 trillion rupiah ($4.85 billion) when the Indonesian crude price was $70/b and could approach 100 trillion rupiah when prices averaged $90-$100/b.
-- Anita Nugraha

Platts Asian LPG Daily Commentary
North Asia LPG prices drop after three-session rally
China's Huayi buys 46,000 mt of propane
Hanwha, Formosa Ningbo cancel LPG tenders
CFR North Asia LPG prices fell at the close of Asian trading Oct. 15, ending a three-session rally, pressured by lower frontmonth Brent futures and strong selling interest in the market.
One bid and three offers were heard during the trading session Oct. 15, but no trades were concluded at the market close.
Platts assessed CFR North Asia propane and butane cargoes at $978/metric ton and $1,053/mt, respectively, on Oct. 15, down $19/mt and $14/mt from the previous session.
Platts assessed CFR South China propane at $995/mt on Oct. 15, up $5/mt day over day, taking into consideration an awarded tender heard delivered to Qinzhou, Guangxi, South of China.
China's Huayi Group bought 46,000 mt of propane for November delivery at a premium of $120s-$130s/mt to the November Far East Index.
Platts assessed CFR East China propane at $1000/mt on Oct. 15, up $7/mt from the previous session.
Platts assessed the CFR North Asia propane-to-C&F Japan naphtha front-month swap spread at minus $11.75/mt on Oct. 15, narrowing from minus $16/mt in the previous session.
In tenders, South Korea's Hanwha TotalEnergies canceled its butane tender for Nov. 1-30 delivery to Daesan, South Korea.
Meanwhile, Taiwan's Formosa Industries (Ningbo) canceled its Nov. 7 propane tender for delivery to Ningbo, eastern Zhejiang province, China. The reason for both tender cancellations was not known.
Australia's LPG exports rose 12.72% from a month earlier to 405 million liters in August, extending the increase from July, the latest preliminary data from the Department of Climate Change, Energy, the Environment and Water showed. Year over year, Australia's LPG exports rose 8.64% in August, after two straight months of decline.
The bulk of Australia's LPG exports, totaling 212.5 million liters, headed to Japan, while China, the country's nextlargest importer, took 130.5 million liters of LPG in August, the data showed.
LPG stocks fell 9.13% from July to 337.6 million liters at the end of August, after two straight months of increases, the data showed. On a year-over-year basis, stocks increased 10.98%, extending the rise from July.
In the US, propane exports continued to rise in September, climbing to a record high of 2.2 million barrels/day, S&P Global Commodities at Sea data showed. The increase from August was driven by exports to Europe, although Asia remained the primary destination for US propane, butane and ethane.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Jeff Ong

West Mediterranean butane stays tight on blending demand
Prices remain supported on tight supply
Turkish refinery output climbs 4.9% monthly
West Mediterranean butane fundamentals remained very tight Oct. 15, with limited spot availability and strong gasoline-blending demand continuing to support higher values.
"The market is still very tight," a market participant said, adding that levels were "moving higher today," reflecting the continued shortage of prompt C4 across the region. Refiners remained focused on internal gasoline blending requirements as winter specifications allowed greater butane use, reducing volumes available to the merchant market.
The lack of visible offers continued to restrict spot activity, while buyers seeking prompt barrels faced limited alternatives. Supportive gasoline-blending economics also encouraged refiners to retain supply for their own pools, thereby keeping regional availability constrained.
With refinery sales limited and import relief not yet sufficient to ease the balance, West Mediterranean butane remained supported by strong seasonal blending demand and a scarce prompt supply.
In refinery news, Turkish refinery production rose to 3.704 million metric tons in August, up 4.9% from July and 7.3% year over year, according to EPDK data.

Tupras' Izmit and Izmir refineries both posted higher output month over month, while the Kirikkale refinery output fell from July but remained higher year over year. Tupras' Izmit refinery has Q4 maintenance scheduled for its fluid catalytic cracker and LPG units, as well as its Platformer and Unifier units.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,161/metric ton, up $5/mt day over day and up 0.50 points as a percentage to naphtha at 126%, or at a $18/mt premium to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.

Tankers
Hormuz traffic drops as Middle East crude exports slip from near-prewar level
Crude exports drop amid heavy maritime attacks
Refine products exports lag behind
Hormuz transits plunge to 12 after container ship sank
Middle Eastern crude and condensate exports have fallen in the second week of October after nearly matching the prewar level in the first week of October, with reduced traffic in the Strait of Hormuz amid an increase in maritime attacks.
Exports have slowed to 11.97 million b/d in the second week of October for crude and condensate and 2.52 million b/d for refined products, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
Crude and condensate exports via Hormuz averaged 18.62 million b/d in the first week of October, equivalent to 98% of the total before the war began in late February, according to CAS.
Refined products exports averaged 3.57 million b/d in the first week of October, 51% of the pre-war total, according to CAS. Shipments have fallen to 2.52 million b/d in the second week of the month.
Combined, oil flows through the chokepoint stood at 88% of pre-war levels in the first week of October, the data shows.
CAS figures may be revised as more data becomes available. Oil exports from the Middle East slumped following the start of the military conflict but have been gradually recovering. In March, total crude and condensate exports amounted to just 7.38 million b/d and refined products 2.18 million b/d, the lowest

Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
LR tankers hold at record highs on supply MR rates diverge across regions
East of Suez Long Range tankers were unchanged but firm at record high levels Oct. 15, on expectations of an increase in supply, and possibility of downsizing of cargoes for use of MRs,

which are relatively cheaper, market participants said. The MRs are declining, and it creates an opportunity to downsize
and split the LR cargoes, if the difference becomes wider, said a chartering executive with a global commodities trading company.
There are a few charterers who are now looking for MRs to do ship-to-ship, or STS transfers of cargoes to be brought out through the risky waters of the Strait of Hormuz, the executive said.
The overall volume of refined products that has transited out from the Persian Gulf is likely to be lower month over month in October due to the escalation of attacks on commercial tankers, he said.
The number of LRs likely to be available for loading during the rest of this month and early November is expected to increase and put downward pressure on the record-high freight, he added.
The LR2 supply is not as tight as is being made out to be because even though close to 100 LR2s have switched to dirty so far this year, there have also been 60 new deliveries and another 22 may be delivered during the fourth quarter, another chartering executive said.
An LR1, the Petrokaravo was heard fixed at w600 for Oct. 20 naphtha loading on STS Sohar-Japan route, with details on the charterer not reported, sources said.
Medium Range, or MRs, saw a divergence across key regions. North Asia MRs were assessed higher due to tight supply amid strong demand to Australia-bound loadings, freight rising above the key psychological mark of w600. A broker said there is still an upward potential.
Southeast Asia MRs climbed higher. A second broker said that sentiment is steady on longer-haul voyages toward Australia, while vessel supply is ample in Southeast Asia. Freight for shorthaul voyages is firm, with at least five Pertamina cargoes loading from Singapore to Indonesia being worked on at the moment, which could provide support to rates, the same broker added.

Persian Gulf MRs slipped day over day, in view of the latest fixtures concluded at lower levels.

Rates for the Americas Medium Range clean tankers showed downward corrections Oct. 15, despite expectations for a firmer sentiment during the final days of the week and continued limited tonnage availability in the US Gulf Coast.

This was followed by another fixture in which Petrobras placed the Forever Conviviality on subjects for an Oct. 24-25 USGC loading run, with an East Coast South America option at w450 and a transatlantic option at w400.
Following this last-done fixture, market calls for both the USGC-Brazil and USGC-Transatlantic runs considered the achieved rates reflective of the market, indicating that rates could now undergo a downward correction amid ballasters heading toward the US Gulf Coast, potentially creating bearish pressure.

US extends Lukoil sanctions waivers for filing stations, Bulgarian assets
Treasury extends transaction permits to April
Funds blocked from transfer to Russian parent
Carlyle out of chase for Lukoil's subsidiary acquisition deal
The US extended its sanctions waivers until April 24 for conducting transactions with LUKOIL International GmbH's overseas filling stations and the company's Bulgarian assets.
The US Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control issued two new licenses on Oct. 15, as the previous waivers were set to expire Oct. 29.
The list of Bulgarian subsidiaries includes the Bulgarian retail arm Lukoil Bulgaria EOOD, the refining business Lukoil Neftohim Burgas, and Lukoil Aviation Bulgaria and Lukoil Bunker Bulgaria.
The waivers allow Lukoil subsidiaries to avoid transaction bans and continue operations. Although the subsidiaries are authorized to receive funds, the assets cannot be transferred to the parent company in Russia, which has been sanctioned by the United States since October 2025.
On Aug. 13, the United Kingdom, which also sanctioned Lukoil in 2025, extended its sanctions waivers for Lukoil's international subsidiary, including its Bulgarian assets, until Feb. 26, 2027.
Since October 2025, all Lukoil's international subsidiaries have required special permissions from the US and the UK to maintain

their operations and negotiate with potential buyers. In January 2026, Lukoil agreed with US investment firm Carlyle
to buy its international subsidiary, but Carlyle did not receive OFAC approval, and the agreement expired at the end of July.
Carlyle told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, that it is no longer "actively pursuing" the purchase of Lukoil's international assets.
Lukoil's international portfolio includes the Shah Deniz project in Azerbaijan, the West Qurna 2 project in Iraq, and upstream assets in Egypt, Cameroon, Nigeria, Uzbekistan, Ghana, Mexico, the UAE and the Republic of Congo. In Romania, Lukoil holds the EX-30 Trident exploration permit in the Black Sea. Lukoil also has stakes in several projects in Kazakhstan.
Lukoil has stakes in a retail network of fuel stations across Central and Eastern Europe, as well as in three European refineries, including Bulgaria's Neftohim Burgas facility, Romania's Petrotel refinery, and the Zeeland plant in the Netherlands, with a combined processing capacity of 400,000 barrels/day.
Despite formally remaining part of Lukoil's international subsidiary, Bulgaria's 190,000 b/d Neftohim Burgas refinery is now under the control of an external administrator. However, there are no plans to nationalize the refinery, according to the Bulgarian government.
-- Alexandra Vladimirova
US Treasury sanctions Iranian oil intermediary in financial crackdown
US targets Iranian oil sales facilitator
Sanctions aim to drain regime's finances
The US Treasury Department issued a new round of sanctions on Iran, including measures targeting an intermediary for oil sales and his two Iran-based companies.
The sanctions are the latest step under Trasruy's so-called Operation Economic Outcast program, aimed at isolate Iran from its trading partners and the global financial system.
"The Department of the Treasury will not relent until the Iranian regime's coffers are empty and every remaining financial lifeline sustaining its war machine has been severed," Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in an Oct. 15 statement.
One target of the new sanctions is Iranian national Sayyed Ruhallah Razavi, son of a hardline leader who Treasury alleges facilitates oil sales and repatriates the revenue for the Iranian regime.
Before the US blockade on Iranian oil exports, Razavi was responsible for shipping millions of barrels of oil overseas each month, the Treasury statement said.
"Razavi has also used his network to move the revenue generated from these oil sales through Iran's clandestine banking system of shell companies and cash and gold couriers," the Treasury statement said.
On Oct. 14, only two noncompliant vessels crossed the US blockade line: one inbound container ship and one outbound

Democratic congressional majorities would ramp oil and gas oversight in 2027: experts
Democrats plan investigations but unable to slow leasing schedule
Federal leasing generates $5 billion in 2026 Bipartisan permitting bill faces divisions
A Democratic majority in one or both chambers of Congress in 2027 would have few direct tools to change the Trump administration's expansion of federal oil and gas leasing, but would likely bring a wave of investigations into the industry's dealings with the White House, legal and policy experts said.
Democrats have already said that energy companies' communications with the administration would be a focus if the party were to win majorities and subpoena power in the Nov. 3 US midterm elections. Any legislation to change the administration's energy agenda would still require the signature of US President Donald Trump.
"Can they actually stop, slow, or investigate? Stop, that's really hard," said Robert Glicksman, a professor of environmental law at George Washington University Law School. "Even if they manage to pass legislation, Trump's going to veto it."
"Of the three, stop, slow, or investigate, it's the investigate that's going to have the most clout," Glicksman said.

"It will be a very active period for congressional investigative activity," Bracewell LLP partner Tom Moyer said.

Lease schedule On his first day back in office, Trump issued Executive Order
14154: Unleashing American Energy, which directed the Interior Department to expand leasing on federal lands and waters. In July 2025, Congress passed Republicans' budget reconciliation package, which required at least 30 lease sales in the US Gulf through 2040 and at least six in Alaska's Cook Inlet. The law also restored the previous offshore royalty rate at 12.5%.
In December 2025, the first Gulf sale under the new law, Big Beautiful Gulf 1, drew just over $300 million in high bids for 181 blocks after a gap in Gulf leasing under the Biden administration, which in 2024 did not hold a single Gulf lease sale for the first time in the history of the Bureau of Ocean Energy Management's offshore leasing program. In 2026, subsequent sales drew $47 million and $82.7 million in March and August, respectively.
Onshore leases have continued at the pace set by the reconciliation bill, with record returns, in large part driven by $2.6 billion worth of tracts purchased in New Mexico's Delaware Basin by Devon Energy in May. The parcels were "directly adjacent" to Devon's existing position in the basin, the company said at the time. The Bureau of Land Management has generated over $5 billion in total receipts from onshore oil and gas leasing in 2026.

US federal oil and gas lease sales

The administration has also sought to push leasing in Alaska's Coastal Plain, both in the National Petroleum Reserve-Alaska and the environmentally controversial and less-developed Arctic National Wildlife Refuge. A June 5 sale in the ANWR's Coastal Plain drew bids on just five of 58 tracts, totaling $3.74 million. The bidders were HEX Energy and the Alaska Industrial Development and Export Authority, a state agency.
Glicksman said ANWR and offshore expansion were the most likely points of friction with a potential Democratic majority. Interior's November 2025 draft proposed offshore program would allow up to 34 lease sales across 21 planning areas, including areas off the coasts of California and Florida. That plan drew opposition from Florida Republicans, including Sen. Rick Scott, who co-sponsored the American Shores Protection Act to extend a federal moratorium on oil and gas drilling off Florida's Atlantic and Gulf coasts.
"Theoretically, you might even get enough Republican buy-in to pass legislation in that area," Glicksman said. Otherwise, he

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

39

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 15, 2026

said, Democrats could argue that renewable energy is becoming cheaper and that continued leasing locks in reliance on oil and gas, while they would be unable to meaningfully slow the pace of the government's leasing program for at least the next two years.
"It's kind of an indirect argument," he said. "They can't stop it."
Oversight to come Congressional committees governed by potential Democratic
majorities would likely start by investigating Trump's energy executive order, said Joe Brazauskas, a partner at Bracewell LLP.
"The president instructed Interior to increase the number of oil and gas leases," Brazauskas said. "Okay, well, I want to find out more about the way that that process was conducted." Committees would then write to companies that may have benefited, asking for "all the documents on this particular topic, all the email communications, the internal communications you've had about this policy," he said.
Democrats have already pledged to investigate the industry's ties to the Trump administration. Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer and Sen. Sheldon Whitehouse, Democrat-Rhode Island, the top Democrat on the Environment and Public Works Committee, said in a Sept. 24 staff report that the oil and gas industry donated $201 million to Trump's reelection and has received an estimated $190 billion. The senators said they conducted 19 separate investigations into US Environmental Protection Agency regulatory decisions alongside other federal agencies, "backed by original analyses of campaign finance filings, corporate earnings, and investor communications."
On Jan. 9, House Oversight Committee Democrats, led by ranking member Robert Garcia, wrote to Chevron, ExxonMobil, ConocoPhillips and Continental Resources. The letters asked about the companies' communications with the administration on Venezuela. On Oct. 1, Garcia said Democrats in the majority would "compel information through subpoenas if needed."
"Any area where there is a perceived benefit gained by a private sector participant, there is going to be potential scrutiny," Moyer said. "It cuts across specific issues and specific companies and specific subsectors within the industry, to the extent of any company that participated in a regulatory process, engaged in communications with this administration, and received a regulatory outcome."
Glicksman said Democrats could also look into whether Trump donors received preferential treatment in leasing. They could also hold hearings on what oil companies knew about climate risks decades ago, an issue raised in the Suncor v. Boulder County case the Supreme Court heard Oct. 5.
In an Oct. 2 memo, law firm Sullivan & Cromwell recommended that "companies with significant federal government interactions and those operating in sectors already receiving congressional scrutiny ... use the coming months to identify their potential exposure and develop a strategic plan for responding."
Moyer added that companies will keep running their businesses and working with their regulators, but "need to do that with eyes wide open."

"[Companies need to be] cognizant of the reality that there's going to be added scrutiny on regulatory processes, on communications around those processes, and the outcomes and the policy justifications," he said.
Permitting bill Glicksman said the largest potential change for industry
may come from a bipartisan permitting bill rather than from oversight.
The American Affordability and Jobs Act was introduced Sept. 30 by Whitehouse, Sen. Martin Heinrich, Democrat-New Mexico, Sen. Shelley Moore Capito, Republican-West Virginia and Sen. Mike Lee, Republican-Utah. It would set a 150-day statute of limitations for certain National Environmental Policy Act challenges, generally bar agencies from revoking issued permits and narrow states' Clean Water Act review authority. According to a release from Heinrich's office, it would also expose administrations that block an entire class of energy projects to legal action and fines. Whitehouse has said a floor vote would likely come during the post-election lame-duck session.
Democrats remain divided over whether the bill would end the administration's holds on wind and solar projects. Rep. Jared Huffman, Democrat-California and the top Democrat on the House Natural Resources Committee, has said the bill offers clean energy developers "nothing to actually approve their projects." He has said Democrats should "see how this election goes" before acting.
Glicksman said the bill's NEPA provisions would mostly benefit fossil fuel projects in the near term.
"At least over the next two years, that's going to be where the action is -- not renewables," he said. If the bill passes and renewable projects move forward, Glicksman said, "you probably will see less Democratic action about fossil fuel projects."
Brazauskas gave his thoughts. "There may be an opportunity to strike deals, particularly if there's a split government or split parties in the House and the Senate," Brazauskas said. "Potentially, we could see legislation move forward. Sometimes, divided government does lead to compromised positions. But I don't necessarily see the administration slowing down just because there is legislation on the table indicating that they ought to maybe take a different policy direction."
-- Eamonn Brennan

Increase applies to both crude and oil products

Argentina's refinery utilization climbs on year to 88.8% in August
Utilization rises from 86.1% in August 2025
Refiners run more crude to boost diesel output
Argentina's refinery utilization rate rose to 88.8% in August from 86.1%in the year-earlier month, as refiners ran more crude to step up diesel production, the state statistics department Indec said on Oct. 15.
August's utilization rate fell from the 86.1% rate reported in July 2026, according to Indec.
The greater use of capacity came after crude runs rose 9.1% on the year in August, led by a 12.4% surge in diesel production and a 5% increase in gasoline output, according to data released earlier in October by Indec.
Demand for petroleum products has been recovering on the whole as the economy grows following a 2023-2024 recession, with growth of 4.4% in 2025 and an expected 1.5% growth in 2026, according to a survey of economists by the Argentine central bank.
A big driver of demand has come from the large agriculture sectors, which are using more diesel to plant the 2026-27 wheat crop and sow fields with 2026-27 corn and sunflowers, while harvesting the 2025-26 corn and sorghum crops, according to data from the Agriculture Secretariat.
State-run YPF has a 55% share of diesel and gasoline sales, trailed by Raizen -- which is being acquired by Mercuria from a joint venture of Cosan and Shell -- alongside BP-backed Pan American Energy and Trafigura.
Argentina has about 560,000 barrels/day of installed refining capacity, but demand generally runs at 550,000 b/d. All the crude is supplied domestically from fields that produced a record high of 932,000 b/d in August. Refiners import additional supplies of higher-grade products due to a lack of capacity to produce them.
-- Charles Newbery
TotalEnergies expects 'exceptional' downstream results for Q3
Refining margins surge to record $33.2/b
Hydrocarbons output rises to 2.5 mil boe/d
Middle East conflict cuts production by 5%
French energy major TotalEnergies SE said Oct. 15 it expects its downstream results for the third quarter to be "exceptional" due to higher refining output and historically high refining margins in Europe and globally.
TotalEnergies expects to report a 10% increase in refining throughput in Q3, which has allowed it "to fully capture" exceptionally high refining margins in Europe and worldwide, the company said in its report on main indicators. The company produced an average of 1.4 million barrels/day across its refining network in Q2.

The company said that in Q3 the European comparative refining margin marker was $33.2/b, more than double the $13.5/b in Q2. TotalEnergies reported a $2 billion increase in refining and chemicals cash flow compared to Q2.
Talking about its hydrocarbons production, TotalEnergies said it expects output at 2.45 million-2.5 million b/d of oil equivalent, up from 2.4 million boe/d in Q2 and roughly in line with 2.5 million boe/d produced in Q3 2025.
The increase in production is attributed to a reduced impact from the Middle East conflict, TotalEnergies said. It clarified that the conflict is expected to affect about 5% of the company's total production.
The company said in July that the Middle East war, which has affected the company's assets in Qatar, Iraq and the UAE, accounted for an 8% annual production loss.
Other output excluding Middle Eastern assets is expected to grow by more than 4% year over year, exceeding the annual guidance of 3%, the company added.
"Exploration & Production results are expected to reflect production growth and the sensitivity to lower oil prices and higher gas prices compared to the previous quarter," TotalEnergies said.
TotalEnergies also said that its oil trading activities continued to outperform in Q3, at a level consistent with the previous two quarters.
CEO Patrick Pouyanne said Oct. 5 that amid recent market disruptions the company has benefited from its structure, which incorporates upstream, midstream, and downstream activities.
"The lesson is that we are entering a new world of disruptions," Pouyanne said. "Funnily, I prefer the world of disruption than the peaceful world. It creates for us more opportunities, but you need to have the tools. You need to have the assets."
TotalEnergies' average liquids price was $87.1/b in the third quarter, down from $91.6/b in Q2.
TotalEnergies is due to release its full quarterly earnings on Oct. 29.

country's stockpiling policy was conceived, the proposed law would raise the government mandate for emergency oil reserves from 40 days of net imports to 60 days. It would also mandate private companies to hold at least 21 days of oil in emergency reserves.
"I think the Middle East crisis has taught us a lot," Godfrey Moagi, CEO of SANPC, said in a speech at the event Oct. 14. "We're going to move to 60 days instead of 40 days, and that is going to be very important to make sure that we secure the energy for this country."
In addition to the higher stockpiling mandate, the government has also proposed changing the composition of its stockpiles to shift away from pure crude reserves. For both public and private reserves counted toward the mandate, at least 30% of inventory should be held in the form of refined products, and storage locations should ideally be moved closer to inland demand centers, according to the government proposal.
South Africa consumes about 27 billion liters of petroleum products every year, and risks a 24 million South African rand ($1.4 million) shock to its economy in the event of a 20% supply shortfall over a two-week period, according to government estimates.
With state-owned reserves concentrated in Saldanha Bay on the country's West Coast, it takes a minimum of 21 days for imports to reach South African ports, and at least another 10 for products to be offloaded and transported inland, according to the estimates.

South Africa aims to formalize new oil stock policy by year-end: CEF
State aims to grow oil reserves from 40 days to 60
Private firms could face mandate for 21-day stockpiles: proposal
Refinery closures create 300,000 b/d fuel deficit: SANPC

South Africa aims to make its new strategic oil stocks policy into law by the end of 2026 after proposing a set of measures that would increase its minimum level of reserves to cover 60 days of net imports, Tshepo Mokoka, CEO of the country's stateowned Central Energy Fund, said on the sidelines of Cape Town's annual African Energy Week conference Oct. 14.
After publishing a proposal text of the new oil stocks law in July, the government is awaiting cabinet approval to greenlight

Refining decline Once the largest refining hub on the African continent, South
Africa was a net fuel exporter until 2006. However, the subsequent closure of its Enref and Sapref
refineries has left it with a 300,000 b/d shortfall in oil products, forcing officials to reassess the country's energy security apparatus, Moagi said at the conference.
In 2024, CEF bought the country's shuttered Sapref refinery

from BP and Shell, and later announced plans to more than double its capacity. The facility has remained offline since 2022, pending a final investment decision on the hundreds of millions of dollars needed to revive the 62-year-old plant.
"We can't expect every country to build a refinery -- we need to build infrastructure cross-border," Moagi said.
An SANPC executive said Oct. 14, the company was closely watching the expansion of Nigeria's Dangote refinery and "sobering" long-term projections for the sector, which leaves significant uncertainty over the competitiveness of new projects.
South Africa introduced a new 25-year leasing agreement in 2025 at its main import terminal, Durban's Island View Precinct, to replace month-to-month arrangements with international energy companies.
International energy companies have been given five years to refurbish tanks at the terminal or build new ones through leasing, according to an executive at state pipeline operator Transnet.
Amid the Middle East conflict, South Africa has pointed to major stockpilers like China, as well as institutions like the International Energy Agency, with its 90-day net import mandate, to help inform its reserves policy, according to its July proposal.
However, the government will rely on private sector participation to help make meaningful storage buildouts, the proposal said.
"This is a burden the state cannot carry alone, especially given fiscal constraints," the Department of Mineral and Petroleum Resources said in its policy proposal.
The ministry proposed using a cost-recovery framework or another financing mechanism to fund new storage, without providing further details.

OIL FUTURES: Prices supported by US crude stock draw to lowest level since July ...from page 1
Distillate inventories fell 2.5 million barrels to total 102.6 million barrels, with USGC driving the move. US Gulf Coast stocks fell 1.8 million barrels to 42.1 million barrels the week to Oct. 9.
Meanwhile, International Energy Agency members will accelerate the release of emergency oil stocks, including more than 20 million barrels of diesel, as tight supplies continue to strain global fuel markets.
The IEA Governing Board said Oct. 15 that about 330 million barrels had been released under its March 2026 Collective Action, and members would take steps to complete the remaining releases of approximately 100 million barrels.
Some members have released more barrels than initially pledged, while others intend to do so, meaning the ultimate draw will exceed the original 400 million-barrel commitment, the agency said.
While global demand continues to pull on US oil supplies, US Energy Secretary Chris Wright pointed to the possibility of easing sanctions on Russian, Venezuelan and Iranian shadow-fleet tankers in a bid to lower freight costs.

Detected and assumed traffic through the Strait of Hormuz fell sharply Oct. 14, with 12 identified transits compared with a revised 27 on Oct. 13, as attention remained focused on the two vessels that were struck during an attempted transit Oct. 14, according to voluntary reports received by the United Kingdom Maritime Trade Operations Centre.
In the Bab al-Mandab Strait, total observed movements increased modestly to 33 vessels from a revised 31 a day earlier, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.

Refinery updates
REFINERY NEWS: Fire occurs at Russia's Ryazan after drone attack
Refinery: Ryazan, Russia
Owner: Rosneft
Overall capacity: 17.1 mil mt/y (342,000 b/d)
A fire occurred at the Ryazan refinery after a drone strike overnight, according to a Telegram post by Ryazan regional governor Pavel Malkov on Oct. 15.
Russia's defense ministry also confirmed the attack on the refinery in a Telegram post.
The refinery was previously hit Sept. 6 and suspended operations and sales on the exchange floor, but subsequently resumed partial operations and sales, according to Russia-based market sources.
Russian Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said Oct. 14 that Ukrainian drones had attacked the Saratov and Ryazan refineries, but Ukraine denied targeting Russian refineries that day.
-- Elza Turner
REFINERY NEWS: Italy's Taranto continues to face supply issues amid blocked access
Refinery: Taranto, Italy
Owner: Eni
Overall capacity: 122,000 b/d
Italy's Eni reiterated Oct. 15 that its Taranto refinery may have to shut down units as inflow and outflow remain blocked.
Access to the refinery has been blocked since Oct. 12 due to union action related to the nearby Ilva steelworks.
It said in a statement that the refinery was operating normally at present, but "critical issues" remain with the supply of technical gases necessary for the functioning of the refinery units, and without them "it will be necessary to schedule a shutdown of the facilities."
The blockades are having "an increasing impact on the distribution of fuel to airports in the Puglia region, as well as to the network of Enilive service stations and those of other

companies," Eni also said Oct. 15. Taranto loads about 260 fuel trucks a day, and sourcing fuel
from other regions "is constrained by long distances and traffic disruptions resulting from the extent of the areas affected by the protests."
Local media have been reporting fuel shortages at retail stations in the regions typically supplied by the refinery, leading to queues and the closures of some stations.
Eni reiterated that a "fuel shortage" remains at the Bari and Brindisi airports and added that more than 150 out of 300 stations in Puglia are out of fuel.
The logistical challenges "inevitably affect the regular shift changes for operational staff" at the refinery, which could "necessitate a reorganization of activities to ensure operational continuity and maintain safety standards at the site."

REFINERY NEWS: India's MRPL Q2 throughput down 5.6% on year
Refinery: Mangalore, South India
Owner: Mangalore Refinery and Petrochemicals Ltd.
Overall capacity: 300,000 b/d
India's state-run refiner Mangalore Refinery and Petrochemicals Ltd recorded a 5.6% year-on-year decline in refined throughput at 4.18 million metric tons (332,000 b/d) for its fiscal second quarter ended Sept. 30, company executives said Oct 15.
Throughput (crude plus other) was also 5.6% lower than fiscal Q1 (April-June), they said.
They attributed the lower processing in the second quarter to the impact of the Middle East conflict.
For the its fiscal first half (April-September), the refiner processed 8.61 million mt (343,000 b/d), the officials said, reflecting 8.3% higher throughput compared with a year ago. MRPL's operations in the first half of the fiscal year reflected uninterrupted refining activities despite geo-political tensions including the Middle East conflict, they said.
MRPL's product portfolio includes naphtha, LPG, motor spirit, high-speed diesel, kerosene, aviation turbine fuel, sulphur, xylene, bitumen along with pet coke and polypropylene, according to the company website.
MRPL processed 16.8 million mt (336,000 b/d) in FY2026 (April-March), down 6.7% a year ago, the ministry said, reflecting the impact of a turnaround in the year-ago fiscal quarter.
MRPL in its fiscal second quarter finalized an agreement for hiring tankages for product storage at Goa for jet fuel and Kochi for gasoil/gasoline, they said.
For South India, MRPL started refined product loading at Aegis terminal in Mangaluru, Hindupur, depot in Andhra Pradesh and Ennore terminal in Tamil Nadu, they said.
MRPL's marketing terminal at Devangonthi, Bengaluru, in South India, obtained International Sustainability and Carbon

Certification-Carbon Offsetting and Reduction Scheme for International Aviation (ISCC CORSIA) certification under the Trader and Storage category for the receipt, storage and blending of sustainable aviation fuel, the company executive said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta
REFINERY NEWS: Malaysia's Port Dickson expects to commission new jetty in Q1 2027
Refinery: Port Dickson, Malaysia
Owner: Petron Malaysia Refining & Marketing
Overall capacity: 88,000 b/d
Petron Malaysia's Port Dickson refinery expects to commission its new jetty in the first quarter of 2027, the company said in multiple stock exchange filings.
The product jetty, which loads the refinery's refined products onto tankers, collapsed partially during a tropical storm on Nov. 28, 2025, which forced the refinery to halt operations, it has said previously.
It also said that after the jetty is replaced, the refinery will commence "full and normal operations."
The company said that in May, the refinery "started limited and intermittent" operations processing its existing crude inventories.
-- Elza Turner
Subscriber Notes
Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

UoM $/mt

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.

Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.
